| `sentence:2.8.1:7ec86c38` | A verifier accepts a member value only when it is exactly that URL for some host, or exactly the origin `https://<host>`, the form of draft -00. |
| `sentence:2.8.1:8d85c3e3` | It compares the value as a string, without normalizing it, and refuses any other value with `403`, including `http`, any port, an uppercase host, a trailing `/`, another path, a query, a fragment, and userinfo. |
| `sentence:2.8.1:ac1cb74a` | An identity is an origin, `https://<host>`, and an application names it by its host, such as `buyer.example`. |
| `sentence:2.8.1:c12e4dd3` | The signer sends that directory URL in the `Signature-Agent` member keyed to the signature's label, with no member parameters, and the signature covers the member as `"signature-agent";key=<label>`. |
| `sentence:2.8.2:0ce0c8db` | `@authority` is that URL's authority. |
| `sentence:2.8.2:8d453a2c` | `@target-uri` is the request URL as it travels on the wire: its WHATWG URL serialization, normalized as RFC 3986 § 6.2.2 describes (lowercase scheme and host, no default port, dot segments removed), without userinfo, a fragment, or an empty query, and with no other character encoded or decoded. |
| `sentence:2.8.2:cf6fab93` | Nobody can replay it as the answer to another request, so a response carries no `nonce`. |
| `sentence:2.8.2:fd94415a` | A responder's `Signature-Agent` names the origin of the host the request was sent to (§ 2.8.1), whatever the request URL's scheme and port. |
| `sentence:2.8.3:0de19395` | A signer also sends `alg="ed25519"`. |
| `sentence:2.8.3:0ea425d8` | A verifier checks each signature with the key the directory provides, and rejects a directory in which an Ed25519 key doesn't sign, a signature whose `created` is more than the 60-second skew allowance in the future, or one whose `expires` has passed. |
| `sentence:2.8.3:53f69938` | Each signing key is an Ed25519 key (`kty` `OKP`, `crv` `Ed25519`), and its `kid` is its JWK thumbprint (RFC 7638; RFC 8037 Appendix A.3). |
| `sentence:2.8.3:838d5bd8` | Each signature covers `"@authority";req` and `content-digest`, and carries `created`, `expires`, the key's thumbprint as `keyid`, and `tag="http-message-signatures-directory"`. |
| `sentence:2.8.3:924ce060` | A verifier fetches a directory over HTTPS only, follows no redirects, and asks for no content coding (`Accept-Encoding: identity`). |
| `sentence:2.8.3:a138a82f` | Covering the request's authority stops another domain from serving the key set again, so a directory server signs for its own configured authority, never for the `Host` a client sends. |
| `sentence:2.8.3:a4687a55` | While a cached copy is less than 5 minutes old, a verifier answers an unknown `kid` from it without fetching again, so a client can't force a fetch with random key ids. |
| `sentence:2.8.3:a46ece5b` | It treats as a failure any status other than `200`, another media type, a `Content-Encoding`, a body that isn't UTF-8 JSON, and a `Content-Digest` without `sha-256`. |
| `sentence:2.8.3:b05b889a` | A party publishes the new key beside the old one and keeps signing with the old key until verifiers' cached copies can refresh (section 5.5.2): at least its directory response's `Cache-Control` `max-age`, since a CDN can serve the old copy that long, plus 5 minutes. |
| `sentence:2.8.3:bebe2fc2` | After a failed fetch, a verifier doesn't fetch that origin's directory again for 60 seconds, and with no cached copy it answers `403` meanwhile (Appendix C.5). |
| `sentence:2.8.3:c4e9602c` | A fetched directory verifies until the earliest `expires` among its signatures, with no skew allowance. |
| `sentence:2.8.3:ee683b3a` | A verifier accepts a signature without `alg`, as in Appendix E.2.3, and refuses any other value. |
| `sentence:2.8.3:f9d8c7c5` | A key directory is a JWK Set served with the media type `application/http-message-signatures-directory+json` (section 5.5.1). |
| `sentence:2.8.4:345665bb` | Resolve `@authority` and `@target-uri` from the verifier's own configured authority, never from the request's `Host`, so a request signed for another server fails. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:353405c3` | Verify the signature and `Content-Digest`. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:4ae468cc` | If one fails, answer `403`. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:4b39a491` | Parse `Signature`, `Signature-Input`, and `Signature-Agent` as Structured Field Dictionaries (RFC 9651 § 3.2). |
| `sentence:2.8.4:52dd2fa8` | If a check fails, answer `403`. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:615ad845` | If one fails to parse, answer `400`. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:771241e7` | Select the `web-bot-auth` member and the `Signature-Agent` member with its label. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:a3f79a4d` | If either is missing or repeated, or the signature doesn't cover the member, answer `403`. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:bc6b56cc` | For a request, check the `nonce` (§ 2.8.5). |
| `sentence:2.8.4:bd4a52e3` | What that origin may do is authorization (§ 1.2, § 5.8), and § 4.1.2 states when the signature attributes an `assent[]` entry to a party. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:ced61d10` | If there is none, answer `403`. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:d0259c60` | Resolve the origin's directory (§ 2.8.3), and select the key whose `kid` equals `keyid`. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:e05ad9a0` | A verifier applies these steps to a message that carries `Signature-Input`: |
| `sentence:2.8.4:e1613972` | A message without `Signature-Input` is unsigned, whatever other signature fields it carries. |
| `sentence:2.8.4:e341d14c` | So does a `Signature-Input` member that isn't an Inner List, a `Signature` member that isn't a Byte Sequence, and the legacy bare-String `Signature-Agent` of section 5.2.1, which this profile doesn't accept. |
| `sentence:2.8.5:0f754399` | It keeps each `nonce` for the signature's remaining lifetime plus the 60-second skew allowance. |
| `sentence:2.8.5:8963e64c` | A verifier that can't check and record the `nonce`, because its store is full or unavailable, answers `503` rather than accept the request (Appendix C.6). |
| `sentence:2.8.5:991bcaae` | `idempotency_key` keeps its role: a repeated `commit` gets the stored result (§ 5.6.1), and the signature covers it through `content-digest`. |
| `sentence:2.8.6:679a5c7a` | These statuses answer the transport, before the request is read, so they carry no `errors[]` (§ 2.4). |
| `sentence:2.8:4fffaeb0` | It then refuses an unsigned request with `403` (§ 2.8.6). |
| `sentence:2:6d07fde0` | The proposal supplies only identity, `spec_version`, the offer window, `version`, and its packages. |
| `sentence:3.1:0a88d171` | Every field marked `selectable` is a `Selectable[T]`, or a named type that holds its keys, where `T` is the option type. |
| `sentence:3.1:1355e230` | § 5.6 states how they enter `agreed_rate`. |
| `sentence:3.1:167f85d2` | `Delta` keys. |
| `sentence:3.1:2da94e5e` | `max_select` counts the whole set. |
| `sentence:3.1:4235f893` | With no `selected[]`, `included[]` is in effect. |
| `sentence:3.1:85278b83` | `Selectable` keys: |
| `sentence:3.1:90082c7b` | `selected[]` is the complete set in effect and replaces `included[]`. |
| `sentence:3.1:ec0ebc8d` | Dropping an option in `included[]` doesn't lower the rate. |
| `sentence:3.2:5abfeb9e` | `Settable` is the envelope form. |
| `sentence:3.2:688cf2cf` | `value` only narrows `allowed[]`. |
| `sentence:3.2:7d7b28fc` | The keys of `commitment` are the one exception: `commitment_bounds.spend` and the committed entry's `unit_bounds` hold its bounds (§ 5.6, § 5.6.1). |
| `sentence:3.2:9509efd3` | `frequency_cap` holds its form in `count`. |
| `sentence:3.2:a0327ab0` | `SettableRange` holds the keys of `Range[T]` (§ 2.2) and these. |
| `sentence:3.2:f07ea411` | `Narrowing` keys. |
| `sentence:3.2:f5da1fc4` | A field marked `settable` declares its bounds in one of two forms, and the buyer writes one key, `value`. |
| `sentence:3.3:27f28f1d` | On a `dooh` line item, they recompute on the screens of the properties in effect, over hours inside both `operating_hours[]` and `daypart`. |
| `sentence:3.3:c10596ed` | A buyer reads these figures by sending the seller an `AvailabilityRead`. |
| `sentence:3.3:d6de45ae` | The read changes no `status`, writes no history, reserves nothing, and doesn't limit a later decline (§ 5.6.1). |
| `sentence:3.3:f4451272` | `AvailabilityRead` holds the keys of `BuyerWrites` (§ 3.4) and these: |
| `sentence:3.4:39df5553` | `BuyerWrites` holds what a buyer writes on one line item. |
| `sentence:3.4:5167ab36` | `BuyerWrites` lists these paths, where `<selectable>` is a field marked `selectable`, and `<settable>` is a field marked `settable` other than `frequency_cap`: |
| `sentence:3.4:808be197` | It is the buyer's writes on one line item, which a `Commit` (§ 5.6.1) and a hold's `requested_terms` (§ 5.6.2) carry. |
| `sentence:3.4:e06f9ab3` | `LineItemWrites` holds the keys of `BuyerWrites` and this one. |
| `sentence:3:786275c2` | § 4.3, not the `seller-set` revision rule, governs the seller's answer to a `requestable` ask. |
| `sentence:3:8bce3258` | Whose `status`. |
| `sentence:3:9cae6039` | `selected[]`, `value`, and `excluded[]` (§ 5.3) are keys of the field and carry no marker of their own. |
| `sentence:3:acf8d54a` | A resolved `commitment` is an offer to buy (§ 5.6.1). |
| `sentence:3:d1ec9803` | The proposal's (§ 4.1) for a field defined in § 4; the line item's (§ 5.1) for a field defined in § 5, including `seller-set` sub-fields of a `mixed` field. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:07a18634` | The only `derived` value inside the record is `pricing[].agreed_rate` on the committed entry. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:085d807a` | In a proposal with `packages[]`, it leaves each package that holds a line item in S, with only the line items and add-ons in S, and each `$.line_items[*]` path above also selects that path under `$.packages[*].line_items[*]` and `$.packages[*].add_ons[*]`. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:0d226a75` | A non-string mapping key, `.inf`, `.nan`, a recursive alias, an explicit tag, or a second document in the stream has no JSON form and makes the document invalid for this section, and so do duplicate object keys in its JSON form. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:219bf348` | Every other value is inside the record, named here or not: `proposal_id`, `spec_version`, `seller`, `buyer`, `type`, `proposal_template_id`, `brief_id`, `issued_from`, `offer_window`, `ext`, and each package's `package_id` and `min_commitments[]`; every `seller-set` field, including `hold_terms` and prose (`description`, `media_kit_detail`, `creative_policy`, `format_specs`); the seller's value of a `requestable` field, such as `pricing[].list_rate`, including one a `grant` or `counter` put in force; every option space (`available[]`, `included[]`, `deltas`, `max_select`, `allowed[]`, `min` / `max`, `default`); every `BuyerWrites` value (§ 3.4) and `commitment`, as they stand at `agreed`; `pricing[].agreed_rate`; and every `DocumentRef` (§ 2.2) whole, with any key its publisher added, whose `url` and `digest` pin the bytes. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:3f083a18` | A `description` that offers an 8.00 `CPM` doesn't change a `list_rate` of 12.00. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:4bba336f` | Restricting to S leaves `line_items[]` an array of the line items in S, in document order however S is named. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:565dfc29` | `record_digest` is the `Digest` (§ 2.2) of the digest input. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:5c177d70` | A stored line item holds the writes of a `commit` only while its `commitment` entry is live or matched by an `acceptance`. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:62bc0de7` | `24.00`, `24`, and the YAML `0x18` are the same value. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:642fcd68` | The one exception is `buyer_supplied`: if it holds no key after these removals, it is removed too, whether the removals emptied it or it was written empty. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:66e6fbb3` | Where a value whose Type cell is `String` (§ 2.6) conflicts with a typed field of the same `version` in the binding record, the typed field governs. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:67ab8f6f` | A `Commit` (§ 5.6.1) carries the buyer's entry and writes, not a copy of the seller's terms. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:6c933da9` | The requirement on creative (`formats`, `format_specs`, `creative_policy`, `materials_due`) is a term, and so is the permission to deliver a list (`segments_allowed`, `suppression_allowed`, `accepted_exclusions`). |
| `sentence:4.1.1:87d9c76f` | Each removed path is one of three things: lifecycle state or sequence position (`version`, `status`, `assent[]`, `negotiation_history[]`, `change_requests[]`, `hold`); a seller assertion, advisory text, an informative figure, or an identifier minted downstream of agreement (`best_for[]`, `not_suitable_for[]`, `seasonality_notes`, `availability`, `addressable_scale`, `expected_match_rate`, `min_rate`, `derived_units`, `external_references[]`); or a delivery (`assets[]`, `segments[]`, `suppression_list`). |
| `sentence:4.1.1:8ca741a7` | The binding record is cut from the composed record: the Full Representation of `version` as the seller published it to this buyer, with the buyer's writes (§ 3.4) applied. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:9f0d6559` | A list the buyer delivers after `agreed` is a delivery and does not change the agreed record (§ 4.1.2). |
| `sentence:4.1.1:9fd49903` | It sits only on `assent[]` entries. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:a91a7ba7` | A value of an `Enum` or of a § 2.2 type, such as an `Instant`, `Duration`, or `VocabularyTerm`, is a typed field. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:b10cb27e` | A `withdrawal` or `decline` acts on the entries it matches (§ 4.1.2), so its `record_digest` is compared, not recomputed. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:b1290f2c` | A document that already holds those writes at that `version` is its own composed record. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:b86f0ddd` | Nothing else narrows: a proposal-level value such as `description` stays as it is. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:ea066674` | A document carried as YAML is a single YAML document, converted to JSON under the YAML 1.2 core schema: aliases expand, and `<<` is an ordinary key. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:ea65ff78` | `derived` values. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:ee465300` | Otherwise, as for a rejected `commit`, a full fetch doesn't show them and they enter no later composed record. |
| `sentence:4.1.1:f8e55774` | On a line item with a `commitment`, a party sets it to the § 5.6 result before serializing, replacing a written value or adding an absent one. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:067e10f7` | Each party keeps the agreed record, and a copy of every document a `DocumentRef` in it pins. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:110168c4` | A line item for which no such pair exists is not agreed, whatever its `status` says. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:18100fa4` | The entry accepts every `terms_refs[]` entry (§ 5.8) of the line items it names, at the bytes each `digest` pins, so a buyer compares each document with its `digest` first. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:248a753f` | To check a pair, a party recomputes `record_digest` from the agreed record, not from the current document, and tests that the two entries match. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:3720a27b` | Each ends the `commitment` entries before it that it matches. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:3d67e37e` | `role: withdrawal` is written by the buyer, and `role: decline` by the seller. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:4185f60b` | `assent[]` holds the parties' acts in the order they were written, and only it decides whether a line item is agreed. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:42dbc7e5` | Adding an `assent[]` entry does not change `version`. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:430978d8` | `agreed` is terminal for a line item. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:46048f7e` | The line items of a `commitment` entry are `agreed` when, and only when, `assent[]` holds a matching `acceptance` entry. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:57dfdeca` | Once a matching `acceptance` exists, nothing ends it. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:5ca39dff` | Once a matching pair exists, an absent or failed signature, or a key later removed from its directory, does not change `agreed`. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:74cbe1c9` | `role: acceptance` is written by the seller, only while a `commitment` entry it matches is live. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:7838c492` | `AssentEntry` keys: |
| `sentence:4.1.2:7c0975a4` | Two entries match when their `version`, `record_digest`, and `line_item_ids[]` are equal. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:8e5481e7` | No check in this specification requires or verifies a signature, and `agreed` does not depend on one. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:aafdf092` | `record_digest` covers `seller` and `buyer`, so the signed body also binds the party's name. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:addc1297` | Requests and responses are both signed, so the seller's `acceptance` and `decline` arrive signed, as the buyer's entries do. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:b1e180e2` | An `acceptance` that matches no live entry is invalid. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:c5c4e5ee` | A `commitment` entry is live until the first of these: a matching `withdrawal` or `decline`, its `commit` going stale (§ 4.1.3) or void (§ 4.3.2, § 4.3.4), or `offer_window.end`. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:c6e43dca` | `line_item_ids[]` compare as sets, so an acceptance cannot cover part of a commitment. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:e95695a5` | `role: commitment` is written by the buyer. |
| `sentence:4.1.2:ec934f27` | The signature attributes the entry to the record's party that `role` implies when it covers the body, through `Content-Digest` (RFC 9530), and the host of the signing origin is that party's `domain` or a subdomain of it, such as `signing.<domain>`. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:031671ad` | `published` for version 1, appended no later than the moment version 1 is frozen, and for a change the seller makes on its own initiative, including a change to `seller-set` prose. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:0efead3c` | A change to the `line_item_id`s of a package that holds a line item it names also makes it stale. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:23e07e1c` | Every other entry (`requested`, `declined`, `withdrawn`) carries the version it refers to. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:2905c906` | `version` is outside the record, so a revision that touches only another line item does not make it stale, and a change to a proposal-level term does. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:379f94d5` | § 5.6.1 states the test for a `commit`. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:3984bae3` | Two representations of the same `(proposal_id, version)` whose offered terms differ, compared on the fields and line items both carry, are non-conformant. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:39b87017` | Its `action` is one of the following: |
| `sentence:4.1.3:52da3817` | The offered terms of a version are every value the seller writes: `immutable` and `seller-set` fields; the seller's value of a `requestable` field, such as `pricing[].list_rate`; the seller-declared structure of `selectable` and `settable` fields (`available[]`, `included[]`, `deltas`, `max_select`, `allowed[]`, `min`, `max`, `default`); and the set of `line_item_id`s the version carries. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:7f809fd0` | An ask or a hold is on a `version`. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:9a678333` | `granted` or `countered` for a change that answers a buyer's `requestable` ask: `granted` puts the requested value into force, `countered` puts a different value into force in its place. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:ee4166ee` | It is stale when the binding record (§ 4.1.1) for the line items it names, with any buyer writes applied, differs between that `version` and the current one. |
| `sentence:4.1.3:fdeb7c34` | A buyer composes against a named `version` and never increments it. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:0ddca2d6` | Whatever `basis` the commitment on `line_item_id` uses, a `spend` threshold reads against its seller total (§ 5.6.5), in the currency of the entry committed (§ 5.6), and a `units` threshold reads against the units that entry commits, as `pricing[].unit_bounds` counts them. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:31c4d5d7` | The holds on the line items in a package's `line_items[]` move as a unit (§ 5.6.2). |
| `sentence:4.1.4:3b37e5bb` | `Package` keys: |
| `sentence:4.1.4:3b9469af` | `MinCommitment` holds the keys of `CommitmentAmount` and this one. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:4cae9eae` | A package can't be bought while a line item in its `line_items[]` is `sold_out` or `withdrawn`, since that line item can't be committed. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:5396c293` | A notice on only some of them, or a request whose notices differ in `cancelled_share` or `requested_effective_at`, exercises no option. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:63dd7c59` | Cancelling the line items doesn't cancel an add-on: a buyer cancels it under its own `cancellation_policy`. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:6bc2a815` | It sends one request (§ 4.4), whose entries share an `id`, with a `cancellation` (§ 5.6.6) for each. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:759ac005` | Each line item keeps its own `status` (§ 5.1). |
| `sentence:4.1.4:86099b57` | Every notice states the same `cancelled_share` and `requested_effective_at`. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:9ad0a9c8` | A line item sold both alone and in a package appears twice, under two `line_item_id`s, as two offers. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:b3c0c7ee` | A proposal that holds `packages[]` holds each of its line items in a package, so a line item the seller sells alone is a package of one. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:b473baac` | `package_id` values are unique within the proposal, and so are `line_item_id` values. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:e1f30211` | Each cancellation takes effect on the latest effective date any of them yields (§ 5.6.6), and cancels no more than the smallest `max_share` that applies on that date. |
| `sentence:4.1.4:ff91db0d` | In an agreed package whose `line_items[]` holds more than one line item, a buyer exercises their `cancellation_policy` options only together. |
| `sentence:4.1:3dab0ceb` | `HistoryPath` keys: |
| `sentence:4.1:49814af1` | A proposal names exactly one `seller.domain`. |
| `sentence:4.1:9ff4638d` | `RequestedValue` holds exactly one key: the `requestable` field `pointer` names, in the form its row states. |
| `sentence:4.1:a0fee758` | `pointer` says which `pricing[]` entry. |
| `sentence:4.1:a8a07485` | `HistoryEntry` keys: |
| `sentence:4.1:da1773b0` | Where a third party hosts a seller's agent or published files, `seller.domain` is the selling entity's own registrable domain, never the host's. |
| `sentence:4.1:fbfad0a5` | For this specification one `seller.domain` is one seller: entities that transact under one domain are one seller here, and `seller.legal_name` carries the legal name. |
| `sentence:4.2:0848d5bd` | They are outside the binding record (§ 4.1.1) and the offered terms (§ 4.1.3), so editing them creates no `version`. |
| `sentence:4.2:e31853ae` | `best_for[]`, `not_suitable_for[]`, and `seasonality_notes` are advisory. |
| `sentence:4.3.2:01ce39af` | The seller rejects it with `stale_version` (§ 2.4). |
| `sentence:4.3.2:1f349c08` | `published` → `under_review`: buyer ask. |
| `sentence:4.3.2:2616bec3` | `published` \| `under_review` \| `sold_out` → `expired`: `offer_window.end` passes (§ 4.3.4). |
| `sentence:4.3.2:263e2d6f` | `published` \| `under_review` \| `sold_out` → `withdrawn`: seller, never while `hold.status` is `held`, nor until the latest `price_valid_until` of its `pricing[]` entries; open asks are void. |
| `sentence:4.3.2:2a216346` | `under_review` → `agreed`: seller `accept` of a `commit` (§ 4.1.2); open asks are void. |
| `sentence:4.3.2:3b027dd8` | A line item moves between `status` values as follows. |
| `sentence:4.3.2:3f09fd6a` | `draft` → `draft` at a new `version`: seller `publish`. |
| `sentence:4.3.2:432cde21` | `draft` → `withdrawn`: seller. |
| `sentence:4.3.2:5b46a741` | The revision is a new `version` (§ 4.1.3). |
| `sentence:4.3.2:62e994af` | It holds until that date or `offer_window.end`, whichever comes first. |

| `sentence:4.3.3:12aff16a` | A proposal's `status` is the first of the following that applies: |

| `sentence:4.3.3:5fa412e4` | `draft` when every line item is `draft`. |
| `sentence:4.3.3:7bc17500` | `published` when any line item is `draft`, `published` or `sold_out`. |

| `sentence:4.3:0172dee2` | A proposal with no `buyer` is shared. |
| `sentence:4.3:0fdeac86` | Asks, `under_review`, holds, and assent live on a proposal issued to one buyer; a shared proposal, or any other record more than one buyer can read (§ 5.8), carries none of them. |

| `sentence:8:a7594c05` | `cancellation` states a notice to cancel. |
| `sentence:8:cb3bf489` | Once one line item guarantees `viewable_rate` and another `completion_rate`, there is no normalised way for a buying agent to compare or optimise across them. 3.0 doesn't cover it. |
| `sentence:8:d62ebb45` | A `spec_ref` names a registry by domain alone: this draft doesn't say which registry that is or how to fetch an entry. |
| `sentence:8:d9620ead` | A `commitment.amount` of 80,000.00 at an `agreed_rate` of 24.00 `CPM` buys 3,333,333 impressions if 24.00 is what the seller invoices. |
| `sentence:8:fc4da301` | Should `billing.payment_terms` and `billing.invoicing_party` be repeated on every line item, given they are usually identical across a seller's shelf? |

| `rule:2.1:2e481f6f` | statement | It MUST contain, per line item: |
| `rule:2.1:2f9d603c` | statement | A full fetch MUST return it. |
| `rule:2.1:8d97860b` | statement | `catalog_ref` values MUST remain unresolved in a summary. |
| `rule:2.1:948e2b02` | statement | A catalog response MAY return it. |
| `rule:2.2:06ccee9f` | statement | When a proposal reaches status `agreed`, every `catalog_ref` MUST be replaced by its resolved value in the stored record, so the committed artifact is self-contained and immutable. |
| `rule:2.2:3916a8f2` | statement | References MUST be version-pinned. |
| `rule:2.2:696ee27b` | statement | A field MUST carry either an inline value or a `catalog_ref`, never both. |
| `rule:2.2:bee40541` | statement | References MUST resolve during discovery. |
| `rule:2.2:c610e803` | statement | Any field whose value is drawn from a seller-maintained catalog MAY carry a reference in place of an inline value: |
| `rule:2.2:fd522468` | statement | A catalog entry MUST be immutable once referenced; a change creates a new version. |
| `rule:2:2ea1196f` | statement | A buyer MAY select and commit a subset of a proposal's line items, unless the proposal explicitly mandates linked execution through its binding terms. |
| `rule:2:7d544674` | statement | Each line item MUST be fully self-contained. |
| `rule:3.1:f3c3cab7` | statement | Every field marked `selectable` MUST declare: |
| `rule:3.1:f8b0c287` | statement | A field marked `selectable` that carries a bare value and no `available[]` MUST be treated as `seller-set`. |
| `rule:3.1:ff8838af` | statement | MAY be empty |
| `rule:3.2:4f3b0a9c` | statement | A field marked `settable` that carries a bare value and no bounds MUST be treated as `seller-set`. |
| `rule:3.2:ee009430` | statement | Every field marked `settable` MUST declare its bounds: either `allowed[]` (an enumerated set) or `min` / `max` (a numeric or date range). |
| `rule:3.3:f58949fc` | statement | Because audiences, properties and formats are `selectable`, `addressable_scale` and `availability` MUST be recomputed against the buyer's actual selection. |
| `rule:3:b51cedb6` | statement | An unset `selectable` or `settable` field takes its declared `included[]` or default, and that default MUST be explicit in the proposal. |
| `rule:3:e768cdf1` | statement | The seller sets it and MAY revise it while status is `draft` or `published`. |
| `rule:4.2:86398bec` | statement | REQUIRED. |
| `rule:5.6.1:e250f467` | statement | A request carrying an `idempotency_key` already seen MUST return the original result and MUST NOT create a second commitment. |
| `rule:5.6:d539a004` | statement | OPTIONAL. |
| `rule:5.7:4994173c` | statement | Seller declares a ceiling; a buyer MAY request above it |
| `rule:5.9:d510ffe5` | statement | A seller MAY offer the same inventory through more than one |
| `sentence:1.1:9c7bf74f` | sentence | Each line item lands in the one its `transaction_mechanism` maps to: |
| `sentence:1:1b060817` | sentence | Custom proposal — built in response to a buyer's agent brief, referenced via `brief_ref`. |
| `sentence:2.1:0fb18f29` | sentence | Full Representation — every field, with all `catalog_ref` values resolved. |
| `sentence:2.1:1f55a1be` | sentence | plus, from the parent proposal: `proposal_id` · `seller_name` · `description` · `valid_from` · `valid_until`. |
| `sentence:2.1:5e6cb5f0` | sentence | `line_item_id` · `channel` · `content_detail` · property names · `formats.included[]` · `pricing.cost_method` · `pricing.gross_rate` · `min_spend` · `addressable_scale` · `committed_metrics[]` · `transaction_mechanism` |
| `sentence:2.2:77e63626` | sentence | A `catalog_ref` is not inheritance. |
| `sentence:3.1:49d63f3e` | sentence | `pricing` generalises it to every dimension a seller is willing to vary, and replaces it. |
| `sentence:3.1:817b18c0` | sentence | `audience_pricing` in earlier drafts was the precedent for this pattern — it expressed "if you change X, the price moves by Y". |
| `sentence:5.5:4869abaa` | sentence | A bare value means the field is `seller-set`. |
| `sentence:5.6.1:bb93abda` | sentence | Frozen when status becomes `agreed`. |
| `sentence:5.6.2:516faad1` | sentence | Transitions: `none` → `requested` → `held` → (`expired` \| `released` \| `converted`). |
| `sentence:5.6.2:774f52b4` | sentence | `converted` is terminal and occurs when the line item reaches `agreed`. |
| `sentence:6.2:0ea01d1d` | sentence | Shows `selectable` audiences with per-option pricing, a catalog reference, a buyer commitment, and `settable` bounds. |
| `sentence:7:256da220` | sentence | Channel support cost one new field and one generalisation — `channel`, and `committed_metrics[]` as tuples — rather than a schema fork per channel. |
| `sentence:7:41ebd0f7` | sentence | `description`, `specifications`, `best_for` and `seasonality_notes` are what a model needs to decide whether to look further. |
| `sentence:8:01a13e05` | sentence | `specs` delegates to a registry that cannot currently answer for half the declared channels. |
| `sentence:8:0bda15d9` | sentence | Do seller catalogs (`catalog_ref`) belong in 3.0, or should the object stay fully inline until a later version? |
| `sentence:8:3549c05c` | sentence | Is a `min_line_items` field needed on the proposal to require a bundle purchase, or is that left to buyer discretion? |
| `sentence:8:4a7b806f` | sentence | `environments[]` needs validation against real inventory. |
| `sentence:8:4b17719a` | sentence | Once one line item guarantees `viewable_rate` and another `completion_rate`, there is no normalised way for a buying agent to compare or optimise across them. |
| `sentence:8:f134c883` | sentence | Should `payment_terms` and `invoicing_party` be catalog references rather than repeated on every line item, given they are usually identical across a seller's shelf? |

| `appendix-b:derived` | appendix_b | `derived` \| 11 \| Computed. Neither party sets it directly. | `derived` \| 3 \| Computed by a rule this specification states. Neither party sets it directly. |
| `appendix-b:immutable` | appendix_b | `immutable` \| 6 \| Set once at creation. Neither party may change it. | `immutable` \| 10 \| Set once at creation. Neither party may change it. |
| `appendix-b:mixed` | appendix_b | `mixed` \| 2 \| Sub-fields carry different markers; see the section. | `mixed` \| 5 \| The keys of the object carry different markers: in the Mutability column of its key table, in its lead-in for composed keys, or on its rows for `pricing[]`. |
| `appendix-b:requestable` | appendix_b | `requestable` \| 3 \| Buyer may ask outside the declared space. Requires seller assent. | `requestable` \| 6 \| The buyer MAY ask for a value outside the declared space. An ask changes the value only through a seller `grant` or `counter` (§ 4.3.1). A change on the seller's own initiative follows § 4.1.3. |
| `appendix-b:selectable` | appendix_b | `selectable` \| 12 \| Buyer chooses from a declared set. Resolves without assent. | `selectable` \| 16 \| The buyer chooses from a seller-declared set. Each option MAY carry a price delta. Resolves without a round trip. |
| `appendix-b:seller-set` | appendix_b | `seller-set` \| 29 \| Seller sets and may revise while `draft` or `published`. | `seller-set` \| 45 \| The seller sets it and MAY revise it while the `status` of the object that carries it is `draft`, `published` or `under_review`. § 4.3.2 states what a revision does to a line item `under_review`. The buyer cannot change it directly. |
| `appendix-b:settable` | appendix_b | `settable` \| 5 \| Buyer sets a value within declared bounds. Resolves without assent. | `settable` \| 4 \| The buyer supplies a value within seller-declared bounds. Resolves without a round trip. |
| `appendix-b:supplied` | appendix_b | `supplied` \| 3 \| Buyer provides data or an artifact. | `supplied` \| 1 \| The buyer provides data or an artifact. The seller does not pre-populate it. |
| `code:2:1` | code_block | Proposal → the merchandising pitch, identity, lifecycle state and assent Line Items[] → fully self-contained buyable units, each in exactly one channel | Proposal → the merchandising pitch, identity, lifecycle state and assent packages[] → optional: sets of line items sold only together line_items[] → buyable units, each in exactly one channel |
| `concept:1.1:1` | concept | Direct buy \| OpenDirect 2.1 · Order + Line | `id` \| `Deal.id`, `Bid.dealid` \| None: the ID in `deal_origin`'s namespace. The `external_references[]` entry with `reference_type: deal` records it (§ 5.10) |
| `concept:1.1:2` | concept | Programmatic guaranteed \| OpenDirect 2.1 + Deals API · `deal_id` | `origin` \| `deal_origin` |
| `concept:1.1:3` | concept | PMP deal \| Deals API + OpenRTB 2.6 · Deal | `seller` \| The proposal's `seller.domain` |
| `concept:1.1:4` | concept | Open market \| OpenRTB 2.6 · open auction, no deal | `wseat` \| `Deal.wseat` \| `buyer_requirements.seats.ids[]` |
| `field:4.1:negotiation_history[]` | field | `negotiation_history[]` \| Array[Object] \| derived \| Ordered, append-only. `{ version, actor: buyer \| seller, action: proposed \| countered \| accepted \| declined \| withdrawn, fields_changed[], at }`. Entries MUST NOT be edited or removed | `negotiation_history[]` \| Array[HistoryEntry] \| recorded \| Ordered, append-only. Entries MUST NOT be edited or removed. A commit and its answers are in `assent[]`, not here, and an entry here voids nothing |
| `field:4.1:proposal_id` | field | `proposal_id` \| String \| immutable \| Unique identity for the proposal object | `proposal_id` \| String \| immutable \| Identifies the proposal. Unique within `seller.domain` |
| `field:4.1:status` | field | `status` \| Enum \| derived \| `draft` \| `published` \| `under_review` \| `agreed` \| `withdrawn` \| `expired` | `status` \| ProposalStatus \| derived \| Computed from the line items (§ 4.3.3) |
| `field:4.1:type` | field | `type` \| Enum \| immutable \| `standard` \| `custom` | `type` \| Enum \| immutable \| `standard` \| `custom` |
| `field:4.1:version` | field | `version` \| Integer \| derived \| Monotonically increasing revision number | `version` \| Integer \| derived \| Monotonically increasing revision number, starting at 1, held by the seller. Each version is a snapshot of the proposal's offered terms (§ 4.1.3). A version is frozen at the earliest of: the proposal's `status` leaving `draft`; a `negotiation_history[]` or `assent[]` entry naming it; a counterparty receiving it in any representation (§ 2.1). The offered terms of a frozen version MUST NOT change; a change to them creates version `n+1`. The values § 4.1.3 lists as not offered terms change without a new version |
| `field:4.2:best_for[]` | field | `best_for[]` \| Array[String] \| seller-set \| Advisory planning hints. Non-normative, and never the basis for a match | `best_for[]` \| Array[String] \| seller-set \| Advisory planning hints. Non-normative, and never the basis for a match |
| `field:4.2:description` | field | `description` \| String \| seller-set \| REQUIRED. Prose, not a name. Where the "why buy" case is made, including which channels a multi-channel proposal spans | `description` \| String \| seller-set \| Prose, not a name. Where the "why buy" case is made, including which channels a multi-channel proposal spans |
| `field:4.2:not_suitable_for[]` | field | `not_suitable_for[]` \| Array[String] \| seller-set \| Advisory exclusion guidance. Non-normative | `not_suitable_for[]` \| Array[String] \| seller-set \| Advisory exclusion guidance. Non-normative |
| `field:4.2:seasonality_notes` | field | `seasonality_notes` \| String \| seller-set \| When the proposal performs differently | `seasonality_notes` \| String \| seller-set \| When the proposal performs differently |
| `field:5.10:billing` | field | `billing` \| Object \| seller-set \| `{ supplier_id, billing_supplier_id, payment_terms, invoicing_party }`. Who delivers is not always who invoices | `billing` \| Billing \| seller-set \| Who delivers is not always who invoices |
| `field:5.10:external_references[]` | field | `external_references[]` \| Array[Object] \| derived \| `{ system, namespace, id, role }`. Replaces platform-specific key names. A buyer-owned reference such as a Prisma `io_id` is supplied | `external_references[]` \| Array[ExternalReference] \| recorded \| The ids other systems hold for this line item. The party that learns an id writes the entry. Once the deal exists in `deal_origin`, the seller MUST record its ID as one `deal` entry on the line item. An entry is not a term and MAY be added after `agreed` |
| `field:5.1:channel` | field | `channel` \| Enum \| immutable \| `display` \| `video` \| `audio` \| `ctv` \| `dooh` \| `native`. The discriminator for how the rest of the line item is read | `channel` \| MediaChannel \| immutable \| Core terms in § 5.1.1. The discriminator for how the rest of the line item is read |
| `field:5.1:line_item_id` | field | `line_item_id` \| String \| immutable \| Identity within the proposal | `line_item_id` \| String \| immutable \| Identity within the proposal |
| `field:5.1:status` | field | `status` \| Enum \| derived \| `draft` \| `published` \| `under_review` \| `agreed` \| `withdrawn` \| `sold_out` \| `expired`. Independent of the proposal's status, so one line item may sell out while the rest of the shelf stays live | `status` \| LineItemStatus \| lifecycle \| Independent of the proposal's status, so one line item may sell out while the rest of the shelf stays live |
| `field:5.2:environments` | field | `environments` \| Object \| selectable \| `available[]` from `web_desktop` \| `web_mobile` \| `app_mobile` \| `ctv` \| `dooh` \| `audio_streaming` | `environments` \| Selectable[Environment] \| selectable \| The distribution channels and device types the line item runs on. See 5.2.1. Absent: the seller promises no environment |
| `field:5.2:properties` | field | `properties` \| Object \| selectable \| `available[]` of `{ id, name, domains, supported_formats }`, `included[]`, `pricing`, `max_select` | `properties` \| Selectable[PropertyOption] \| selectable \| What the line item runs on. See 5.2.4. Absent: the seller promises no property |
| `field:5.3:activation_route` | field | `activation_route` \| Object \| selectable \| `available[]` from `publisher_side` \| `clean_room` \| `id_partner`, `included[]`, `pricing` | `activation_route` \| Selectable[VocabularyTerm] \| selectable \| Where the audience is matched. Core terms: `seller_side` (the seller's systems) \| `clean_room` (a clean room) \| `id_partner` (a third-party identity provider) |
| `field:5.3:activation_route#description/values:1` | value_list | `publisher_side` \| `clean_room` \| `id_partner` | `seller_side` (the seller's systems) \| `clean_room` (a clean room) \| `id_partner` (a third-party identity provider) |
| `field:5.3:addressable_scale` | field | `addressable_scale` \| Integer \| derived \| Reachable units in the flight, recomputed against the buyer's selection. Not segment size | `addressable_scale` \| AddressableScale \| asserted \| What the line item can reach, recomputed against the buyer's selection. Not segment size. See below |
| `field:5.3:audiences` | field | `audiences` \| Object \| selectable \| `available[]` of `{ id, name, taxonomy_ref, provenance, addressable_scale }`, `included[]`, `pricing` as `delta_cpm` per option, `max_select` | `audiences` \| AudienceSelectable \| selectable \| See below |
| `field:5.3:buyer_supplied` | field | `buyer_supplied` \| Object \| supplied \| `{ segments_allowed, suppression_allowed, match_rate_expected, segments[], suppression_list }`. `match_rate_expected` is the seller's stated expectation; `segments[]` and `suppression_list` are buyer-populated | `buyer_supplied` \| BuyerSupplied \| mixed \| See below |
| `field:5.4:approval` | field | `approval` \| Object \| seller-set \| Review SLA and revision policy | `approval` \| String \| seller-set \| Review SLA and revision policy, in prose |
| `field:5.4:assets[]` | field | `assets[]` \| Array[Object] \| supplied \| Buyer-delivered. `{ asset_id, format_ref, url_or_tag, submitted_at, approval_status }` | `assets[]` \| Array[Asset] \| mixed \| A record of the creative that reaches the seller or that the seller builds |
| `field:5.4:creative_delivery` | field | `creative_delivery` \| Object \| seller-set \| `{ method: buyer_hosted \| seller_hosted \| api \| platform_managed, protocol, secure_required, endpoint }` | `creative_delivery` \| CreativeDelivery \| seller-set \| How creative travels. See the creative path below. Absent: this line item states no path, and a `materials_due` still applies |
| `field:5.4:formats` | field | `formats` \| Object \| selectable \| `available[]` of registry format ids namespaced by channel (`display/300x250`, `audio/host-read-30s`), `included[]`, `pricing`, `max_select` | `formats` \| Selectable[FormatId] \| selectable \| Format ids, such as `display/300x250` and `audio/host-read-30s`. Absent: this line item states no format |
| `field:5.4:materials_due` | field | `materials_due` \| Timestamp/Offset \| requestable \| Deadline for creative to land. A buyer MAY request a later date | `materials_due` \| MaterialsDue \| requestable \| Deadline for creative to reach the seller. A buyer MAY request a later date |
| `field:5.4:production` | field | `production` \| Object \| selectable \| Custom units. `available[]` of build options with `pricing` and `lead_time`; `{ custom_unit, build_owner, lead_time, cost }` | `production` \| Selectable[ProductionOption] \| selectable \| Custom units. See `ProductionOption` below. Nothing in effect: no custom unit |
| `field:5.5:daypart` | field | `daypart` \| Object \| settable \| `allowed[]` of time windows | `daypart` \| Settable[ClockWindow] \| settable \| `allowed[]` of `ClockWindow` (below). A window covers any window inside it |
| `field:5.5:device` | field | `device` \| Object \| settable \| `allowed[]` of device categories | `device` \| Settable[Integer] \| settable \| `allowed[]` of AdCOM 1.0 List: Device Types codes, the values of OpenRTB 2.6 `device.devicetype`. It narrows `environments` (§ 5.2.1). Mobile/Tablet - General (1) covers Phone (4) and Tablet (5) |

| `field:5.5:geo` | field | `geo` \| Object \| settable \| `allowed[]` of geo codes the buyer may select within | `geo` \| Settable[GeoCode] \| settable \| Where the line item runs. `allowed[]` of `GeoCode` (below). A line item MUST NOT declare both `geo` and `markets` |
| `field:5.6:availability` | field | `availability` \| Object \| derived \| `{ unit, basis, as_of, granularity, type: forecast \| reservable, quantity }`. Recomputed against the buyer's selection | `availability` \| Availability \| asserted \| What the seller says it can deliver, recomputed against the buyer's selection (§ 3.3). See below |
| `field:5.6:cancellation_policy` | field | `cancellation_policy` \| Object \| selectable \| `available[]` of cancellation rights with `pricing` per option | `cancellation_policy` \| Selectable[CancellationOption] \| selectable \| See § 5.6.6. See 5.6.1 |

| `field:5.6:pricing[].currency` | field | `pricing[].currency` \| String \| seller-set \| ISO-4217 | `pricing[].currency` \| CurrencyCode \| seller-set \| The currency of the entry's rates (below) |

Distinct from the proposal's `valid_until` | `pricing[].price_valid_until` \| Instant \| seller-set \| How long the entry holds (§ 4.3.2). Distinct from the proposal's `offer_window.end` |

Seller declares a ceiling; a buyer MAY request above it | `committed_metrics[]` \| Array[CommittedMetric] \| requestable \| `CommittedMetric` tuples (§ 5.7.1). Seller declares its offered `level`. A buyer MAY request a stricter `level`, one further in `direction`. A request MUST NOT change a tuple's `direction` |

Nothing in effect: no provider |
| `field:5.7:reporting` | field | `reporting` \| Object \| selectable \| `available[]` of `{ feed_type: log_level \| aggregate \| none, cadence, delivery_mechanism, metrics_provided[], latency }` with `pricing` | `reporting` \| Selectable[ReportingOption] \| selectable \| See § 5.7.1. Nothing in effect: no report is promised |
| `field:5.7:shortfall_remedy` | field | `shortfall_remedy` \| Object \| selectable \| `available[]` from `makegood` \| `credit` \| `none`, plus `remedy_terms` | `shortfall_remedy` \| ShortfallRemedy \| selectable \| Core terms: `makegood` \| `credit` \| `none`. See `remedy_terms` in § 5.7.1. Nothing in effect: this line item states no remedy. Its committed levels stay guaranteed, because only `none` makes them targets |
| `field:5.7:shortfall_remedy#description/values:1` | value_list | `makegood` \| `credit` \| `none` | `makegood` \| `credit` \| `none` |

| `field:5.9:serving_mode` | field | `serving_mode` \| Enum \| seller-set \| `client_side` \| `ssai` \| `dai` | `serving_mode` \| VocabularyTerm \| seller-set \| How the ad reaches the content or the player. Core terms: `dai` \| `scheduled` \| `embedded`, defined below. `placement.ssai` states server-side insertion |
| `field:5.9:serving_mode#description/values:1` | value_list | `client_side` \| `ssai` \| `dai` | `dai` \| `scheduled` \| `embedded` |
| `field:5.9:serving_platform` | field | `serving_platform` \| String \| seller-set \| Where the line item serves | `serving_platform` \| Domain \| seller-set \| The ad platform that serves the line item, such as the seller's ad server, by its domain. For a deal sold through a separate SSP it differs from `deal_origin` |
| `field:5.9:transaction_mechanism` | field | `transaction_mechanism` \| Object \| selectable \| `available[]` from `DIRECT_IO` \| `PROGRAMMATIC_GUARANTEED` \| `PREFERRED_DEAL` \| `PRIVATE_AUCTION` \| `OPEN_AUCTION`. A seller MAY offer the same inventory through more than one | `transaction_mechanism` \| Selectable[MechanismOption] \| selectable \| The mechanisms the seller offers. A seller MAY offer the same inventory through more than one. `max_select: 1` |
| `marker:3:derived` | marker | `derived` \| Computed from other fields. Neither party sets it directly. | `derived` \| Computed by a rule this specification states. Neither party sets it directly. |
| `marker:3:requestable` | marker | `requestable` \| The buyer MAY ask for a value outside the declared space. Requires explicit seller assent. | `requestable` \| The buyer MAY ask for a value outside the declared space. An ask changes the value only through a seller `grant` or `counter` (§ 4.3.1). A change on the seller's own initiative follows § 4.1.3. |
| `marker:3:selectable` | marker | `selectable` \| The buyer chooses from a seller-declared set. Each option MAY carry a price delta. Resolves without seller assent. | `selectable` \| The buyer chooses from a seller-declared set. Each option MAY carry a price delta. Resolves without a round trip. |
| `marker:3:seller-set` | marker | `seller-set` \| The seller sets it and MAY revise it while status is `draft` or `published`. The buyer cannot change it directly. | `seller-set` \| The seller sets it and MAY revise it while the `status` of the object that carries it is `draft`, `published` or `under_review`. § 4.3.2 states what a revision does to a line item `under_review`. The buyer cannot change it directly. |
| `marker:3:settable` | marker | `settable` \| The buyer supplies a value within seller-declared bounds. Resolves without seller assent. | `settable` \| The buyer supplies a value within seller-declared bounds. Resolves without a round trip. |
| `rule:3:07a16404` | statement | `selectable`, `settable` and `supplied` fields MUST resolve without a round trip — everything needed to validate and price them is declared in the proposal. | `selectable`, `settable` and `supplied` fields MUST resolve without a round trip — everything needed to validate and price them is declared in the proposal. |
| `rule:4.1:1a0e1d65` | statement | REQUIRED when `type` is `custom` | REQUIRED when `type` is `custom` |
| `rule:4.1:8e9aacb8` | statement | Entries MUST NOT be edited or removed | Entries MUST NOT be edited or removed |
| `sentence:3:cb1810ae` | sentence | Only `requestable` fields require the counterparty to respond. | Only `requestable` fields require the counterparty to respond. |

## Schema changes

The release differ (`src/release.ts`) run as if `with-our-stack` were the next release after `main`. `breaking`: a payload valid on `main` may be invalid with `with-our-stack`. `additive`: it accepts or defines more. `annotation`: only prose changes. Each view lists a change it shows, so one change can appear in more than one view.

### Model (697)

## Statements

Where the model places each statement: a rule on its owner, an obligation on a party, a statement a type's schema expresses, or an example of an object.

| `def:1.1:transaction_mechanism.direct_io` |  | schema TransactionMechanism | `direct_io` \| OpenDirect 2.1 · Order + Line \| `order`, `line` \| An OpenDirect 2.1 ChangeRequest on the booked Line (`lineid`). `change_requests[]` (§ 4.4) records it against the baseline `VersionRef` (§ 2.2). A partial cancel uses ChangeRequestLine, and a full cancel uses Line `?cancel` at the effective date |
| `def:1.1:transaction_mechanism.preferred_deal` |  | schema TransactionMechanism | `preferred_deal` \| Deals API 1.0 · `Deal` + OpenRTB 2.6 · `Deal` \| `deal` \| By agreement between implementers, against the same baseline. A full stop is `sellerstatus` 4 (archived) |
| `def:1.1:transaction_mechanism.private_auction` |  | schema TransactionMechanism | `private_auction` \| Deals API 1.0 · `Deal` + OpenRTB 2.6 · `Deal` \| `deal` \| As for `preferred_deal` |
| `def:1.1:transaction_mechanism.programmatic_guaranteed` |  | schema TransactionMechanism | `programmatic_guaranteed` \| OpenDirect 2.1 · Order + Line, as the reservation; Deals API 1.0 · `Deal` \| `order`, `line`, `deal` \| The reservation: as for `direct_io`. The deal: as for `preferred_deal` |
| `def:2.2:Amount` |  | schema Amount | `Amount` \| Number within the limits of § 4.1.1 \| Money. It carries no currency: § 5.6 states which one it reads in |
| `def:2.2:ApprovalStatus` |  | schema ApprovalStatus | `ApprovalStatus` \| `PENDING` \| `APPROVED` \| `REJECTED` \| An approval outcome, in the spelling of OpenDirect 2.1's ChangeRequest `status` |
| `def:2.2:CalendarUnit` |  | schema CalendarUnit | `CalendarUnit` \| `hour` \| `day` \| `week` \| `month` \| A calendar bucket |
| `def:2.2:Digest` |  | schema Digest | `Digest` \| String of 71 characters: `sha256:` followed by the 64 lowercase hexadecimal characters of the SHA-256 of its input \| The value carries the algorithm, and a field name never names it. `sha256` is the only algorithm in this version. Each field that holds a `Digest` states its input |
| `def:2.2:Domain` |  | schema Domain | `Domain` \| String: lowercase ASCII, internationalized labels in A-label form (RFC 5890), with no scheme, port, path, or trailing dot \| Producers MUST write this form. Consumers MUST compare byte for byte and MUST NOT normalize a value before comparing it. A value read from ads.txt or sellers.json is converted to this form before comparison |
| `def:2.2:Duration` |  | schema Duration | `Duration` \| String: the ISO 8601 grammar of RFC 3339 Appendix A without its week form (`P14D`, not `P2W`) \| A length of time. A comparison names its anchor. A length within the playback of an ad or a placement, such as a play, view, read, loop, slot, or refresh, is instead an Integer whose key ends `_s` or `_ms`, except a key imported from another standard, which keeps its spelling (§ 2.6) |
| `def:2.2:EntryRef` |  | schema EntryRef | `EntryRef` \| String: `<domain>/<collection>/<entry_id>@<version>`, with no `/` or `@` inside a part \| A named entry at one exact version, whose content does not change. `<domain>` is a `Domain`. It carries no digest |
| `def:2.2:Ext` |  | schema Map | `Ext` \| `Map[Domain, Object]` \| The extension container. § 2.5 states its rules |
| `def:2.2:FieldPointer` |  | schema FieldPointer | `FieldPointer` \| String: a JSON Pointer (RFC 6901) \| A location in the object this specification defines |
| `def:2.2:Fraction` |  | schema Fraction | `Fraction` \| Number from 0 to 1 \| A proportion |
| `def:2.2:FractionDelta` |  | schema FractionDelta | `FractionDelta` \| Number greater than -1 \| Used only by `delta_fraction` (§ 3.1) |
| `def:2.2:HttpsUrl` |  | schema HttpsUrl | `HttpsUrl` \| A `Url` whose scheme is `https` \| A location fetched over TLS |
| `def:2.2:IanaZone` |  | schema IanaZone | `IanaZone` \| String: a name in the IANA time zone database, such as `America/New_York` or `UTC` \| A time zone |
| `def:2.2:Instant` |  | schema Instant | `Instant` \| String: an RFC 3339 `date-time` in whole seconds, ending in `Z` \| A point in time. One written form, so one instant has one digest input |
| `def:2.2:IntegerFlag` |  | schema IntegerFlag | `IntegerFlag` \| Integer: `0` (no) \| `1` (yes) \| A yes or no that another standard writes as an integer. Each field says what `1` affirms |
| `def:2.2:IntegerFlag#form/values:1` |  | schema IntegerFlag | `0` (no) \| `1` (yes) |
| `def:2.2:LineItemStatus` |  | schema LineItemStatus | `LineItemStatus` \| A `ProposalStatus`, or `sold_out` \| A line item's `status`, moved as § 4.3.2 states |
| `def:2.2:Measurer` |  | schema Measurer | `Measurer` \| A `Side`, or a `RegistrableDomain` \| Who counts or measures. A seller writes `seller`, never its own domain. Two values name one party only when they are equal byte for byte. A `qualifier` beside a value names a product or method of that party and is not compared |
| `def:2.2:ProposalStatus` |  | schema ProposalStatus | `ProposalStatus` \| `draft` \| `published` \| `under_review` \| `agreed` \| `withdrawn` \| `expired` \| A proposal's `status`, computed by § 4.3.3 |
| `def:2.2:RegistrableDomain` |  | schema RegistrableDomain | `RegistrableDomain` \| A `Domain` that is the Public Suffix List registrable domain, taken over the whole list, PRIVATE section included \| It names a business |
| `def:2.2:Side` |  | schema Side | `Side` \| `buyer` \| `seller` \| It says which side, never who that side is. `Party` says who |

| `rule:2.5:b7667a2c` | MAY | obligation implementation | The proposal, each line item, and each `change_requests[]` entry (§ 4.4) MAY carry `ext`, an `Ext`. |
| `rule:2.5:c97afe58` | MAY | obligation implementation | Apart from an unknown term (§ 2.2), a receiver MAY skip a whole line item it cannot read, without assenting to it. |
| `rule:2.5:d8749eb6` | MUST NOT | obligation consumer | A receiver MUST ignore an `ext` entry it does not understand, and MUST NOT reject a document because of it. |

| `rule:2.8.6:60100c34` | SHOULD | obligation implementation | On a `403` or `429`, a party SHOULD send `Accept-Signature` (RFC 9421 § 5.1) naming the components and parameters of § 2.8.2, and on a `503` it sends `Retry-After` instead. |
| `rule:2.8.7:bb44896e` | MUST NOT | obligation implementation | The private keys in its `keys.json` are public test keys: section 6.8 says they "MUST NOT be used in production", and a verifier SHOULD reject them. |
| `rule:2.8:909151f9` | MAY | obligation implementation | A party MAY require a signature on the routes it serves. |
| `rule:2.8:cc948a48` | OPTIONAL | obligation implementation | Signing is OPTIONAL in this specification: § 4.1.2 states what a signature shows, and `agreed` never depends on one. |

| `rule:3:e1b586de` | MUST NOT | obligation buyer | The buyer MUST NOT alter the keys the seller declares beside them. |
| `rule:3:e5ff81ed` | MAY | obligation seller | The seller sets it and MAY revise it while the `status` of the object that carries it is `draft`, `published` or `under_review`. |
| `rule:4.1.1:6e3d2c89` | MUST NOT | obligation implementation | A producer MUST NOT write a Number that this serialization does not reproduce as the numeric value written; a Number with at most 15 significant digits inside the range of a double always does, and a document containing one that does not is invalid for this section. |
| `rule:4.1.1:e93caddf` | MUST | obligation consumer | A receiving party MUST recompute `record_digest` and compare it byte-for-byte before relying on a live `commitment` entry, over the line items as they stand plus the writes its `commit` carries (§ 5.6.1), or on a matching pair, from its agreed record (§ 4.1.2). |

| `rule:4.1.2:a855dcd7` | MUST | obligation implementation | Every `seller-set`, `selectable`, `settable`, and `requestable` value of it and its `pricing[].agreed_rate` MUST remain as in the version `assent[]` names. |

| `rule:4.1.2:b3c1ac3b` | OPTIONAL | obligation implementation | The evidence is OPTIONAL. |
| `rule:4.1.2:c9ce18dd` | MAY | obligation implementation | An entry that is not live stays in `assent[]`, and the line items it names MAY appear in a new `commitment` entry. |

| `rule:4.1.2:dc1eb3b2` | MUST NOT | obligation implementation | A line item MUST NOT be named by more than one live `commitment` entry, nor by more than one `acceptance` entry. |
| `rule:4.1.3:03718402` | MUST | obligation implementation | Each change to the offered terms of a frozen version MUST create version `n+1` and MUST append exactly one `negotiation_history[]` entry carrying that `version`, with `actor: seller` and `paths[]` naming every offered-terms field whose value differs from version `n`. |
| `rule:4.1.3:862b74db` | MUST | obligation seller | A seller MUST keep every version that a `negotiation_history[]` or `assent[]` entry names retrievable by `(proposal_id, version)` for as long as the proposal is retrievable. |
| `rule:4.1.3:dd69dac7` | MAY | obligation seller | A seller MAY edit a proposal in place only while its current version is not yet frozen (§ 4.1). |
| `rule:4.1.3:e06c57e1` | MUST NOT | obligation implementation | A line item that appears in a frozen version MUST NOT be removed from the proposal or moved to another package; it leaves the shelf by its `status` (§ 5.1), which is a lifecycle change. |
| `rule:4.1.3:fb70b3cb` | MAY | obligation implementation | The following are not offered terms and MAY change without a new version: `status` (proposal and line item), `assent[]`, `negotiation_history[]`, `change_requests[]`, `availability`, `addressable_scale`, `hold`, `external_references[]`, `assets[].approval_status`, a `built_by: seller` entry of `assets[]`; `best_for[]`, `not_suitable_for[]`, and `seasonality_notes`, which are advisory (§ 4.2); `pricing[].min_rate`, which is a hint; `buyer_supplied.expected_match_rate`, which is the seller's stated expectation; every value the buyer writes (a `BuyerWrites` value, `commitment`, any other `supplied` field, and a `requestable` ask); and every `derived` value computed from a buyer's writes (`pricing[].agreed_rate`, `commitment.derived_units`). |
| `rule:4.1.4:24987106` | MUST | obligation implementation | A `commit` that names an add-on MUST name every line item in its package's `line_items[]` and meet each of the package's `min_commitments[]`. |

| `rule:4.3.2:29fded3f` | MAY | obligation seller | A seller MAY revise a line item while an ask is open or a hold runs. |
| `rule:4.3.2:31f66e42` | MUST | obligation implementation | An ask it makes stale ends (rule 1), and the seller MUST release a `held` hold it makes stale or re-offer it at the new `version`, where `hold.status` stays `held`. |
| `rule:4.3.2:4825c8da` | MUST NOT | obligation implementation | While it holds, a seller revision MUST NOT change that entry, `pricing_id` included, and MUST NOT end it sooner by moving `offer_window.end`. |
| `rule:4.3.2:72de0cc2` | MUST | obligation seller | The seller of an issued proposal MUST answer a resolved `commit` under it that arrives while the entry holds, on or after `offer_window.start`, and passes the checks of § 5.6.1 and any authority check the seller applies (§ 5.8): accept it, or decline it on an allowed ground. |
| `rule:4.3.2:84b5ebab` | MUST NOT | obligation implementation | A `commit` MUST NOT carry a `requestable` ask: `accept` makes no new `version`, so another value takes a separate `request`. |
| `rule:4.3.2:d044b92c` | MUST | obligation implementation | The answer MUST come before the entry stops holding: otherwise silence works as a decline on no ground. |
| `rule:4.3.4:6f1237a1` | SHOULD | obligation implementation | `status` unchanged; a later `commit` under that entry is rejected (§ 5.6.1), and the seller SHOULD `publish` or `withdraw` |
| `rule:4.3.4:e97d564c` | MUST NOT | obligation implementation | `hold.valid_until` MUST NOT exceed `offer_window.end` or the earliest `pricing[].price_valid_until`. |

| `rule:5.4.1:11d3ac1e` | MAY | obligation implementation | Synthetic events MAY be sent to test endpoints, or through a test mode that the receiving service documents as excluding those events from production measurement and delivery diagnostics. |
| `rule:5.4.1:3a29e732` | MUST NOT | obligation implementation | An implementation MUST NOT report a check that was skipped or could not finish as passed. |
| `rule:5.4.1:52bc805d` | MUST NOT | obligation implementation | The inspector MUST NOT request a destination or redirect that sends a production notification; it MAY use a separate inspection URL or the receiving service's documented test mode under the conditions below. |
| `rule:5.4.1:725c4a7c` | MUST | obligation implementation | If the service cannot provide such a session, the creative MUST be inspected outside the stitched stream. |
| `rule:5.4.1:7adf48a7` | MUST NOT | obligation implementation | An implementation performing inspection MUST NOT send any production notification. |
| `rule:5.4.1:9a1bf7bd` | MUST | obligation implementation | A preview served through server-side ad insertion MUST use a session in which the insertion service sends no production notifications. |
| `rule:5.4.1:ef1a453e` | MAY | obligation implementation | An implementation MAY retrieve creative documents, follow VAST wrappers and inspect referenced media as needed for its checks. |
| `rule:5.4.1:f25ad098` | MUST | obligation implementation | A preview that executes creative or verification code MUST prevent that code from sending production notifications. |
| `rule:5.4.1:fbc60b60` | MUST | obligation implementation | Inspection failures MUST be reported through diagnostics, without firing a production VAST `<Error>` or `verificationNotExecuted` URL. |
| `rule:5.4:28521712` | MAY | obligation implementation | A `spec` MAY also hold `voice`, a `VocabularyTerm` that says whether a synthesized voice may read the ad. |

| `sentence:2.2:db4f2290` |  | schema Range | At least one bound is present, and `min` is no greater than `max`. |
| `sentence:3.1:0a88d171` |  | schema Selectable | Every field marked `selectable` is a `Selectable[T]`, or a named type that holds its keys, where `T` is the option type. |
| `sentence:3.2:f5da1fc4` |  | schema Settable | A field marked `settable` declares its bounds in one of two forms, and the buyer writes one key, `value`. |
| `sentence:5.6.1:78efd2ce` |  | rule LineItem | A `commitment` resolves under § 3 when the buyer is eligible (§ 5.8), every `selected[]`, `excluded[]`, and `value` on the line item is within what the seller declared (§ 3.1, § 3.2, § 5.3), and the commitment fits `commitment_bounds.spend` and the committed entry's `unit_bounds`, is not stale (§ 4.1.3), and meets the package rules (§ 4.1.4). |
| `sentence:5.6.5:03d899cb` |  | rule LineItem | When `commitment.basis` is `units`, `derived_units` is `amount`, and the seller total is `amount` ÷ rate per × `agreed_rate` plus those charges. |
| `sentence:5.6.5:10182d60` |  | rule LineItem | When `calendar_zone` is present, `derived_units` is instead the number of calendar days in that zone from the day of `commitment.flight.start` through the day of `commitment.flight.end`. |
| `sentence:5.6.5:4804c1fd` |  | rule LineItem | `derived_units` = (`amount` − those charges) ÷ `agreed_rate` × rate per, rounded down to a whole unit, where rate per comes from the table for a core term and from `rate_per` for an extension term. |
| `sentence:5.6.5:aa43e614` |  | rule LineItem | `FLAT` and `TIME` set these figures from the rate and the flight instead, and a rule that reads `commitment.amount` on such an entry reads them: the seller total when `commitment.basis` is `spend`, `derived_units` when it is `units`. |
| `sentence:5.6.5:d956510e` |  | rule LineItem | `derived_units` is the time from `commitment.flight.start` through `commitment.flight.end`, inclusive, in `rate_unit`s rounded to the nearest whole unit, half up, so a clock change inside the flight doesn't change it. |
| `sentence:6.10:2d916e96` |  | example LineItem | See `examples/line-item-podcast-embedded.yaml`. |
| `sentence:6.11:71be7fff` |  | example LineItem | See `examples/line-item-ctv-live-series.yaml`. |
| `sentence:6.12:b569c32e` |  | example Proposal | See `examples/proposal-issued.yaml`. |
| `sentence:6.5:5a32cf98` |  | example Proposal | See `examples/proposal-package.yaml`, and the bonus alone in `examples/line-item-ctv-bonus.yaml`. |
| `sentence:6.6:70160ee0` |  | example ProposalManifest | See `examples/manifest.json`. |
| `sentence:6.7:d6388541` |  | example LineItem | See `examples/line-item-rewarded-video.yaml`. |

### Cited only with `with-our-stack` (17)

| Decision | Status | Question |

| D-048 | proposed | Is each type the text writes as a list of values, in a § 2.2 row such as `Side` or in a sentence such as "`HoldStatus` is `requested` \| …", a closed vocabulary? |
| D-049 | proposed | Where the stack's text defines a type the kernel already holds under that name in another form, as § 2.2's `Digest`, which form does the stack's schema take? |
| D-050 | proposed | How do the stack's § 3.1 `Selectable[T]` and § 3.2 `Settable[T]`, `Narrowing[T]`, and `SettableRange[T]` map, where the kernel's `Selectable` and `Settable` hold other keys? |
| D-051 | proposed | Which type does each type the stack defines in its own words take, where the other locks' texts write the same form under another name, or none? |
| D-052 | proposed | Which type does a row the stack types only as `String`, `Integer`, `Number`, or `Object` take, where no shared type and no list gives it another form? |
| D-053 | proposed | How do the stack's `VocabularyTerm` rows and lists map: their term grammar, their names, and whether each is open? |
| D-054 | proposed | What are the § 2.2 types whose Form adds a value or a type to another, a line item's status, a time unit, and a measurer? |
| D-055 | proposed | Does each `role` of a § 4.1.2 `AssentEntry` make a type of its own? |
| D-056 | proposed | What are the stack's `LineItemSummary`, `ProposalSummary`, and `BuyerWrites`, which a lead-in says list paths of a document? |
| D-057 | proposed | Which of the stack's RFC 2119 statements outside the tables does a schema check, and which bind a party? |
| D-058 | proposed | What do the stack's prose lists, examples, and tables that restate or illustrate another definition map to? |
| D-059 | proposed | What is an `ExclusionList`, the value of each `exclusions` key? |
| D-060 | proposed | How do the stack's presence conditions that a schema can't state, such as "REQUIRED when the line item offers a deal mechanism", read, and what payloads does each accept? |
| D-062 | proposed | § 2.2 says a range's `min` is no greater than its `max`, and § 5.9 types `retention` as a range of durations. When is one duration no greater than another, given that `P1M` and `P30D` can be the same length or not? |
| D-063 | proposed | § 5.6.5 gives `action_type` the core terms of another standard: ECAPI 1.0's `event_type` values, from its Standard Events and Additional Events tables both, other than `custom`. Which terms does the vocabulary hold, and what does its schema accept? |
| D-064 | proposed | § 2.2 types `IanaZone` as "a name in the IANA time zone database". Which names does a schema accept, from which release of the database, and which engines check them? |
| D-065 | proposed | § 5.6.5 states how a commitment's figures follow from its entry and its flight in sentences with no RFC 2119 keyword, and § 5.6.1 that a commitment resolves only when it fits its bounds. Which of them are rules one line item decides, and what does each check? |
